I bought this special cuticle oil that's supposed to help heal them, it smells like lavender and chamomile. Lavender is supposed to be really calming, right? I tried drinking lavender tea before bed once, but it honestly just tasted like soap. Like I was drinking bathwater. My grandmother used to keep those little fancy soaps shaped like seashells in her guest bathroom, and we were never allowed to use them. They just sat in this little glass dish collecting dust for like, ten years. I always thought that was so funny, having soap you can't use. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, as a kid? I don't really know if I was hyper. I mostly just remember the attention stuff and the talking too much.\n\nDoctor: Did your difficulties with attention and impulsivity cause significant problems in your social relationships or occupational functioning before the age of 12?\n\nPatient: Problems before I was 12? Um... I don't really think so? I mean, I got those notes on my report cards like I said, but I still got decent grades, and I had friends. It wasn't causing major issues or ruining my life. My best friend back then was Melissa, and we were basically inseparable. We used to spend hours in her basement making up these elaborate dance routines to Paula Abdul tapes. \n\nHer basement had that really dark wood paneling that was everywhere in the 80s? My husband actually brought up putting wood paneling in our den recently, like that modern shiplap stuff you see all over HGTV. I watch way too much of that channel. I can sit there for three hours on a Sunday just watching couples argue over kitchen islands and backsplash tiles. I honestly don't get the obsession with white subway tile, it always just makes me feel like I'm in a public restroom or an actual subway station. It's so cold looking. I prefer warmer tones in a house, especially since I look at spreadsheets all day for work. You need a break from the stark white, you know? I've been trying to convince him to let us paint the den a nice sage green instead. \n\nBut, um... what was I saying? Oh, right, childhood. Yeah, no, I don't remember the attention stuff causing any huge problems with my friends or school back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with attention or impulsivity in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely happening everywhere now. Like, at work, I'm making these silly mistakes, and I just forget to follow up on things. It actually got bad enough recently that a client review was affected, which was mortifying. I'm up for a promotion, and now I'm terrified they think I can't handle it. Which is so frustrating because I know the material! I've been doing this for almost twenty years. I started right after I got my bachelor's. My first job was at this tiny firm where the boss insisted on using this archaic software from the 90s. It was all green text on a black screen, like The Matrix or something. Have you seen that new Matrix movie they made a while ago? I tried to watch it on a flight to Denver, but I kept falling asleep. I can never sleep on planes unless I have the window seat so I can lean against the wall. The middle seat is just torture. Last time I flew, the guy next to me was eating a tuna sandwich. Who brings a tuna sandwich on a plane? It's just inconsiderate. \n\nBut um, yeah, so work is stressful. And at home, it's not much better. My husband has basically had to take over paying all the bills and handling commitments I said I'd do, just because he can't count on me to actually finish them. He's been really patient, but I know it frustrates him. He got me this nice planner for my birthday to help, it has these beautiful leather covers. The leather smells so good, like a new car. I love that new car smell. They sell air fresheners that are supposed to smell like it, but they always just smell like chemicals to me. \n\nSo, yeah... work and home are both pretty messy right now because of all this.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or behave impulsively in multiple settings, like both at home and in school, when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Multiple settings when I was a kid? Um... I honestly don't remember it being an issue at home. Like, my mom never complained about me not listening or running around or anything. At school, yeah, I talked a lot, but at home I think I was pretty normal? I spent a lot of time in my room reading, actually. I loved those Babysitters Club books. I had almost the entire collection lined up on my bookshelf in numerical order. I was obsessed with Claudia because she was the artsy one and hid junk food in her room. I tried hiding a bag of Doritos under my bed once, but we ended up getting ants. It was a whole disaster. My mom had to call the exterminator, and he was this really tall guy who smelled strongly of peppermint. Apparently, peppermint oil is a natural deterrent for ants and spiders. I actually started putting peppermint oil on cotton balls in my garage last fall because I saw a huge wolf spider near the lawnmower. It was terrifying. I hate spiders so much. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, I don't think I had those issues in more than one place back then. It was mostly just the talking in class.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience significant symptoms of inattention, such as making careless mistakes or difficulty sustaining attention, before the age of 7?\n\nPatient: Before I was 7? Um, well... yeah, actually. Even way back in early elementary school, my report cards always said the same thing. They'd say I was bright, but I was always losing my assignments or rushing through things and missing the small details. I talked out of turn a lot, too, and the teachers constantly had to remind me to actually finish my work. My mom still jokes about it, saying I could ace a test but completely forget to turn in the project that was sitting right there in my backpack. \n\nBackpacks were so different back then, weren't they? I had this bright pink one with a neon green buckle. It was very 80s. I think I got it at a department store that went out of business years ago. Mervyn's, maybe? I loved going to Mervyn's with my mom. They had this little section near the registers with those weird, squishy toys that stuck to the wall if you threw them. I bought one once, and it left this awful greasy stain on the wallpaper in our hallway. My dad was so mad. He spent a whole weekend trying to scrub it off, and eventually, he just had to paint over it. He chose this awful mustard yellow color that completely clashed with the carpet. I don't know why he thought mustard yellow was a good idea. \n\nAnyway, um... yeah, so the careless mistakes and not paying attention, that was definitely happening even when I was really little.\n\nDoctor: Did your difficulties with attention and impulsivity significantly impact your ability to make and keep friends as a child?\n\nPatient: Make and keep friends? Um, no, not really. I mean, I always had a pretty solid group of friends when I was a kid. We'd ride our bikes everywhere, you know? I had this purple Huffy with a banana seat and those little tassels on the handlebars. I loved that bike. We used to ride down to the corner store and buy those candy necklaces and Pop Rocks. Do they even make Pop Rocks anymore? I remember there was this urban legend that if you ate Pop Rocks and drank soda at the same time, your stomach would explode. We were so scared of that, but my brother dared me to do it once. I was terrified, but obviously, nothing happened. My brother was always daring me to do stupid things. He once dared me to eat a spoonful of hot sauce, and I thought my tongue was going to burn off. I had to drink like three glasses of milk. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, friends... no, I didn't have trouble making friends. I was pretty outgoing, so that part was fine.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience significant symptoms of hyperactivity, such as fidgeting or restlessness, in multiple settings, like both at home and in school, when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Hyperactivity in multiple settings? Um... no, I really don't think so. Like I said, at home I was pretty quiet, mostly just reading in my room. I wasn't running around or bouncing off the walls or anything like that. I did have a lot of energy when I was outside, though. We had this huge oak tree in our front yard, and I used to spend hours trying to climb it. My dad built a treehouse up there eventually, but before that, I would just sit on this one thick branch and pretend I was a lookout on a pirate ship. \n\nPirates were a big deal for me for a while. I watched that movie Hook with Robin Williams probably a hundred times. He was so good in that. It's so sad what happened to him. I actually read this article a while back about how a lot of comedians struggle with depression. It makes sense, I guess, kind of like the tears of a clown thing. They make everyone else laugh, but inside they're hurting. I went to a comedy club once in Chicago, Second City, and the performers were just brilliant. They do all this improv, and it's amazing how fast they think on their feet. I could never do that. I'm an auditor, so everything I do is very structured and by the book. Spreadsheets, compliance checks, that kind of thing. \n\nBut, um... wait, what was the question? Hyperactivity? Yeah, no, not at home. Maybe a little fidgety at school sometimes, but definitely not in multiple places.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever told that you had problems with paying attention or behaving in more than one place, like both at home and in school, when you were little?\n\nPatient: More than one place? Um... I'm not really sure. I don't think anyone ever sat me down and told me it was an issue everywhere. Like I said, I just remember the report cards. My parents would go to those parent-teacher conferences to talk about them, the ones they held in the school gymnasium with all the little folding tables set up. \n\nThat gym always smelled exactly the same—like industrial floor wax and rubber dodgeballs. I absolutely hated dodgeball. I was always trying to blend into the bleachers so I wouldn't get targeted by the older kids. Our gym teacher took it way too seriously, too. He'd blow his whistle so loud it would make my ears ring for the rest of the day. I actually still get ringing in my ears sometimes. Though, honestly, that might be from my old Walkman. I used to blast my mixtapes on that thing. Making a mixtape was a real art form, you know? You had to sit by the radio for hours just waiting for your favorite song, and then hit record at the exact right second before the DJ started talking over the intro. It was so stressful! Kids today have absolutely no idea, they just click a button on Spotify and it's done. My niece made a playlist for our family road trip last summer in about three minutes, and it had like fifty songs on it. \n\nWait, sorry, I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Oh, right, being told I had problems at home and school. Yeah, no, I don't really remember anyone saying that to me. Just the school stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Social situations as a teenager? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I had my friends from when we were younger, and we just kind of stuck together through middle school and high school. We spent a lot of time hanging out at the mall. That was the thing to do back then, you know? Just walking around the mall for hours on a Saturday. 

We used to go to this one music store, Sam Goody, I think it was called? Or maybe Tower Records. I can't remember, but they had this huge wall of CDs. I bought my first CD there, actually. It was Alanis Morissette, *Jagged Little Pill*. I played that album so much I think I wore it out. My parents hated it because I'd play it on my boombox in my room, and the bass would rattle the floorboards. My dad was always coming up the stairs and telling me to turn it down because he was trying to watch the news. He was obsessed with the evening news, like he couldn't miss a single broadcast or the world would end. He'd sit in his La-Z-Boy recliner with his dinner on a TV tray. I haven't seen a TV tray in years. Do people still use those? Now everyone just eats on their couches while looking at their phones, I guess.

It had these little enamel pins all over it. I was obsessed with collecting pins for a while. I actually just found a box of them in my attic when I was looking for our Halloween decorations. We go all out for Halloween in my neighborhood, it's wild. My husband bought one of those twelve-foot skeletons for the front yard this year, and it took us three hours to put it together. We had to use a step ladder, and the wind caught it and it almost fell over onto the neighbor's rose bushes... I would have felt so bad, Mrs. Higgins loves those roses. She's out there pruning them every single morning at, like, 6 AM. I don't know how anyone functions that early.\n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? Oh, right, when I was younger. Yeah, my mom and the teachers definitely noticed I had trouble paying attention and staying quiet back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I really do. I catch myself jumping into conversations before people even finish their sentences. It happens a lot with the junior auditors at work. It's just... the answer or the idea feels so urgent in my head, like I have to get it out right that second, and I just blurt it out before I can even stop myself. \n\nI actually did it to one of the new hires yesterday. His name is David, he just graduated from State. He's a really smart kid, but he talks so slowly. Like, painfully slowly. He was trying to explain how he reconciled this one account, and I already knew where he was going with it by the second sentence, so I just cut him off and finished it for him. Which I know is rude, and I always feel bad about it later. I think I made him nervous. \n\nSpeaking of State, they're having a terrible football season this year, aren't they? My husband went there, and he is just miserable every Saturday. We usually have people over to watch the games, make some chili or something. I make this really good white chicken chili, I use those little green chiles from the can? It gives it such a good flavor. But lately, nobody even wants to come over to watch because the team is losing so badly. It's kind of a bummer, because I really love having an excuse to make all those fun appetizers. I bought this new chip and dip tray from Target that's shaped like a football stadium, and I haven't even gotten to use it yet. \n\nBut yeah, the interrupting. It's definitely a problem, especially at work. I just have zero filter when an idea pops into my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with distractions, restlessness, and interrupting others causing problems in your relationships or affecting your overall well-being?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It's getting pretty bad, honestly. Like at work, my mistakes and just... not finishing my follow-ups have piled up to the point where a client review was actually affected recently. Which is awful, and now I'm really worried people are questioning whether I can even handle the promotion I've been up for. \n\nThe client was this mid-sized landscaping company, actually. They do really beautiful work. We tried to plant a garden last spring, mostly just tomatoes and some peppers, but the squirrels ate literally every single tomato before they even turned red. Have you ever tried to grow vegetables around here? It is so frustrating. I bought this special netting and everything, but those squirrels are like little ninjas. He just can't count on me to complete them reliably anymore. \n\nLike last month, I was supposed to pay the water bill and renew the car registration, and I completely forgot because I went to find the checkbook and ended up reorganizing the entire home office filing cabinet by color. Which looks amazing, by the way. I found these gorgeous pastel folders at Staples, they're like mint green and lavender. But Tom was not thrilled when the late notice came for the water bill. I think the 90s are coming back in style, which is wild to me because I feel like we just lived through that. Have you seen the jeans the kids are wearing now? The huge, baggy ones? I make my mashed potatoes with cream cheese instead of regular milk, by the way. It makes them so incredibly fluffy. I got the tip from that same Vermont blog I was telling you about. \n\nBut yeah, the attention stuff and blurting things out... it's definitely been a lifelong thing, it just wasn't ruining my life until the promotion.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant difficulties with attention and impulsivity for most of your life, or have there been periods where you didn't have these issues?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's always been there. Like I just said, I mean, even as a kid I was like this. It's not like it ever went away, it just... wasn't as big of a deal when my life was simpler, you know? Like when I was in college, I could just pull an all-nighter if I procrastinated too much. I drank so much Mountain Dew back then, it's a wonder my teeth didn't rot out of my head. Do they still make Code Red? I used to love that stuff. I saw a commercial the other day for some new energy drink that's supposed to be made with mushroom extract or something. Which sounds absolutely disgusting. I like mushrooms on pizza, though. There's this little place down the street from my office that does a wood-fired mushroom and truffle oil pizza that is just to die for. The crust is perfectly crispy. Um, you know, I'm not really sure about that. I don't specifically remember being super hyper or bouncing off the walls or anything. I mean, maybe I was? I fidgeted a little, I guess, but who doesn't? I actually have this terrible habit of picking at my cuticles when I'm sitting at my desk now. I bought this special cuticle oil that's supposed to help heal them, it smells like lavender and chamomile. Lavender is supposed to be really calming, right? I tried drinking lavender tea before bed once, but it honestly just tasted like soap. Like I was drinking bathwater. I don't really know if I was hyper. Before I was 12? Oh, um, well... yes, actually. I mean, my mother still jokes about it. She always says I could ace a test but completely forget about the big project that was sitting right there in my backpack for weeks. Backpacks back then were so heavy, weren't they? Wait, sorry, I'm drifting again. You were asking about before I was 12. Right. Yeah, even in elementary school, my report cards always said the same things. He once dared me to eat a spoonful of hot sauce, and I thought my tongue was going to burn off. I had to drink like three glasses of milk. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, friends... no, I didn't have trouble making friends. I was pretty outgoing, so that part was fine.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience significant symptoms of hyperactivity, such as fidgeting or restlessness, in multiple settings, like both at home and in school, when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Hyperactivity in multiple settings? Um... no, I really don't think so. Like I said, at home I was pretty quiet, mostly just reading in my room. I wasn't running around or bouncing off the walls or anything like that. I did have a lot of energy when I was outside, though. We had this huge oak tree in our front yard, and I used to spend hours trying to climb it. My dad built a treehouse up there eventually, but before that, I would just sit on this one thick branch and pretend I was a lookout on a pirate ship. \n\nPirates were a big deal for me for a while. I watched that movie Hook with Robin Williams probably a hundred times. He was so good in that. It's so sad what happened to him. I actually read this article a while back about how a lot of comedians struggle with depression. It makes sense, I guess, kind of like the tears of a clown thing. They make everyone else laugh, but inside they're hurting. I went to a comedy club once in Chicago, Second City, and the performers were just brilliant. They do all this improv, and it's amazing how fast they think on their feet. I could never do that. I'm an auditor, so everything I do is very structured and by the book. Spreadsheets, compliance checks, that kind of thing. \n\nBut, um... wait, what was the question? Hyperactivity? Yeah, no, not at home. Maybe a little fidgety at school sometimes, but definitely not in multiple places.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever told that you had problems with paying attention or behaving in more than one place, like both at home and in school, when you were little?\n\nPatient: More than one place? Um... I'm not really sure. I don't think anyone ever sat me down and told me it was an issue everywhere. Like I said, I just remember the report cards. My parents would go to those parent-teacher conferences to talk about them, the ones they held in the school gymnasium with all the little folding tables set up. \n\nThat gym always smelled exactly the same—like industrial floor wax and rubber dodgeballs. I absolutely hated dodgeball. I was always trying to blend into the bleachers so I wouldn't get targeted by the older kids. Our gym teacher took it way too seriously, too. He'd blow his whistle so loud it would make my ears ring for the rest of the day. I actually still get ringing in my ears sometimes. Though, honestly, that might be from my old Walkman. I used to blast my mixtapes on that thing. Making a mixtape was a real art form, you know? You had to sit by the radio for hours just waiting for your favorite song, and then hit record at the exact right second before the DJ started talking over the intro. It was so stressful! Kids today have absolutely no idea, they just click a button on Spotify and it's done. My niece made a playlist for our family road trip last summer in about three minutes, and it had like fifty songs on it. \n\nWait, sorry, I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Oh, right, being told I had problems at home and school. Yeah, no, I don't really remember anyone saying that to me. Just the school stuff.\n\nDoctor: Did your difficulties with attention and impulsivity cause any problems for you in social situations when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Social situations as a teenager? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I had my friends from when we were younger, and we just kind of stuck together through middle school and high school. We spent a lot of time hanging out at the mall. That was the thing to do back then, you know? Just walking around the mall for hours on a Saturday. \n\nWe used to go to this one music store, Sam Goody, I think it was called? Or maybe Tower Records. I can't remember, but they had this huge wall of CDs. I bought my first CD there, actually. It was Alanis Morissette, *Jagged Little Pill*. I played that album so much I think I wore it out. My parents hated it because I'd play it on my boombox in my room, and the bass would rattle the floorboards. My dad was always coming up the stairs and telling me to turn it down because he was trying to watch the news. He was obsessed with the evening news, like he couldn't miss a single broadcast or the world would end. He'd sit in his La-Z-Boy recliner with his dinner on a TV tray. I haven't seen a TV tray in years. Do people still use those? Now everyone just eats on their couches while looking at their phones, I guess. \n\nBut, um, anyway, what were we talking about? Social stuff in high school? Yeah, no, I didn't really have any problems there. I was pretty comfortable with my group of friends.\n\nDoctor: Were you told that you had problems with paying attention or behaving in more than one place, such as both at home and in school, before you were 12 years old?\n\nPatient: Before I was 12? Oh, um, well... yes, actually. I mean, my mother still jokes about it. She always says I could ace a test but completely forget about the big project that was sitting right there in my backpack for weeks. Backpacks back then were so heavy, weren't they? I had this bright pink JanSport, and we had to carry all those massive hardcover textbooks because they didn't have laptops or tablets in schools yet. I remember my history book weighed a ton. I loved history, though. Especially ancient Egypt. I was totally fascinated by mummies and the pyramids. We went to a museum exhibit once, I think it was the Field Museum, and they had an actual mummy. It was kind of creepy but so cool. I bought a little scarab beetle necklace at the gift shop and wore it every single day until the clasp broke. I tried to fix it with superglue, but I just ended up gluing my fingers together. That was a disaster. I had to soak my hand in nail polish remover for what felt like hours. The smell of acetone still gives me a headache. \n\nWait, sorry, I'm drifting again. You were asking about before I was 12. Right. Yeah, even in elementary school, my report cards always said the same things. The teachers would write that I was bright, but I constantly lost assignments, or I'd rush past the small details and make careless mistakes. And I apparently talked out of turn a lot and always needed reminders to actually finish my work. So, yeah, I guess it was definitely happening back then, both at school and with my mom at home."
  }
]
